## Nov 22, 2024 | [\[moveable\] Fresno gameplan](https://www.google.com/calendar/event?eid=MnU3azVpdnVicWNlaDVjaWgwYnZwMTZmMGYgaXZhbkBoeXBoYWUubmV0)
Attendees: [Brent Bucknum](mailto:brent@hyphae.net) [Merissa MacDonald](mailto:merissa@hyphae.net) [Ivan Heitmann](mailto:ivan@hyphae.net) [Maxwell Pingeon](mailto:max@hyphae.net)
### **Meeting Notes: Fresno County Extreme Heat Analysis**
**Date:** November 22, 2024  
**Attendees:**
  - Brent Bucknum (BB)
  - Maxwell Pingeon (MP)
  - Ivan Heitmann (IH)
-----
### **Context/Background**
**Project:** Fresno County Extreme Heat Analysis  
**Purpose:** Respond to Fresno COG RFP, focusing on extreme heat vulnerability, urban greening, and community outreach strategies.  
**Deadline:** November 26, 2024  
**Key Requirements:**
  - Extreme heat vulnerability analysis
  - Tree canopy and vegetative cover plan + 5 pilot projects overview 
  - Pedestrian and bike traffic analysis (to also inform the 5 pilot projects) 
  - Working group with CBO representation + wider community outreach research translation campaign
-----
### **Key Discussion Points**
#### ~~**1. Community outreach **~~
  - ~~BB raised the idea of hiring a local project manager, preferably bilingual, to reduce travel costs and handle community outreach in the Central Valley.~~
  - ~~MP clarified his proposed strategy (as outlined in narrative draft):~~
      - ~~Have the designated CBO in Working Group be paid for their Working Group participation from the COG budget ~~
      - ~~But fund from our budget a separate community outreach campaign that translates working group proceedings to the community in a phased approach, ensuring community buy-in, and continuously reporting community concerns back to the Working Group    ~~
          - ~~BB approves~~
#### **2. Budget Constraints and Scope Feasibility**
  - ~~IH and BB both noted the project budget is tight for the ambitious scope; recommends delivering conceptual-level outputs (e.g., project profiles, fact sheets) rather than schematic designs. ~~
      - ~~We need to state explicitly in scope that we are talking about high-level concepts and preliminary costing, not detailed schematic drawings~~
      - ~~IH: “these are like tasks that directly lead from one to the next as a way of starting to develop a set of pilot projects. And the idea would be that you can't change the environment in Fresno County with five projects. And certainly not with like, What they've asked for is project profiles and fact sheets. So they're not asking you for plans or changing the environment. They're asking you for five pilot projects. They're just explaining what the project is about, giving you some facts about it. And that will certainly include some costing. Because they specifically asked for that, right? So you might say like, this is a million dollar project. This is a quarter million dollar project. And this project includes, you know, planting 2,000 trees. This project is like putting a, whatever, putting a shade structure on, you know, Main Street.”~~
      - ~~And that we should build into our narrative our ability to leverage these efforts into future rounds of funding with our grant-writing capacity ~~
  - ~~BB emphasized writing explicitly what in the RFP we think is achievable within budget constraints, using existing models from other projects (STEP, Dallas).~~
      - ~~BB: "We should be direct in the document, communicating what we know and what this budget could probably get... In this other project, we spent 40 grand buying bike and ped data for one neighborhood. So, we should discuss the best data sources and say, 'With this amount of money, we could conceive a strategy or pull existing county-level data, but the way we’d recommend it might need to be a follow-on project.'"~~
      - ~~I.e. ~~~~*paraphrasing*~~~~: “We’ve done this. We have a grant from the State to do exactly this (buying data showing where people are, community mapping, urban greening, urban mobility, connectivity etc)~~
          - ~~With this amount of money cannot be done at large scale ~~
          - ~~But we could come up with a plan for how to scale up the project~~
          - ~~Include a PDF doc of STEP and Dallas etc  ~~
              - ~~Idea is to be direct but also to softly underscore that we have extensive experience in these types of projects and are thus ideally suited to receive the award ~~
  - ~~Budget refinements:~~
      - ~~BB: “We probably can't have 60 grand for some other consultants, unless they're like doing a major lift of the deliverable.” ~~
#### **3. Working Group and Community Partnerships**
  - ~~Fresno COG requires a CBO on the working group ~~
  - ~~MP suggested the Central California Asthma Collaborative (CCAC) as the working group’s CBO, leveraging their air monitoring experience for extreme heat adaptation.~~
      - ~~BB suggested adding Binational California, as recommended by Matt Holmes.~~
          - ~~MP to follow up~~
  - ~~Re: Mona and Tree Fresno~~
      - ~~Mona happy to work on project, especially in its attention to small unincorporated communities (reflecting her prior proposed role in EHCRP proposal) ~~
      - ~~We need to figure out how Mona wants to share workload w/ Mona’s landscape architect  ~~
      - ~~And discuss a budget: How much relationship-building with local communities does she want to be doing (and how to build that into budget) ~~
  - ~~BB: Important to be more explicit about the tasks involved in reporting back to the Working Group~~
      - ~~That usually involves a lot of work that needs to be laid out in line items~~
  - ~~The Review Existing Plans, Guidelines, and Policies should be spun in the narrative as an opportunity to maximize efficiency by seeing areas where Hyphae work could overlap w/ ongoing agency projects~~
  - ~~We also talked about how instead of proposing monthly Working Group meetings, we wanted to do longer quarterly (?) meetings of 3 hours each (rather than monthly meetings where people are unprepared--don't have enough time to put together in the interval)~~
  - ~~        ~~
#### **4. Project Profiles**
  - ~~Project profiles (fact sheets) should include a mix of rural and urban areas, with high-level costing for implementation.~~
  - BB and IH emphasized that as currently drafted, the budget is not realistic: proposing tasks that are in reality more expensive than what they are currently scoped out as   
  - ~~BB suggested seeking out community input with analysis for efficiency’s sake:~~
      - ~~Re: Mona’s contacts in Central Valley, e.g.: ~~
          - ~~"Sometimes when we try to only do that analytically, we can just spin more and more wheels...as opposed to just being like, 'Oh, I know people in this city and they’re down.' That’s sometimes as helpful."~~
  - ~~Confusion as to how the “costing” task could precede the actual selection of the 5 project areas…  ~~
#### **5. Documentation and Visual Aids**
  - IH and BB to prepare (IH on Sunday, DF/BB on Monday/Tuesday) a PDF package showcasing past work (e.g., STEP, Dallas) to demonstrate expertise visually.
-----
### **NARRATIVE Section**
**Value Proposition:  
**Hyphae Design Lab brings unparalleled expertise in combining technical analysis and community-driven planning to address extreme heat vulnerability. Our adaptive strategies empower underserved communities, leveraging data and design to build resilience against climate change.
**Community Benefit:  
**This project addresses environmental justice by prioritizing disadvantaged Fresno communities, ensuring they lead decision-making processes that directly affect their health and well-being.
**Technical Capabilities:  
**Advanced GIS mapping, thermal comfort modeling, and community-centered design tools position Hyphae as a leader in sustainable urban planning.
-----
## Proposed email to send to Mona (11/21/24)
Hi Brent,
I also emailed this to you but are you able to review the email below and tell me if this is kosher to send to Mona?
Many thanks,
Max 
Hi Mona,
Just wanted to follow up on gauging your interest on this [Fresno RFP](https://www.fresnocog.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/RFP_Extreme-Heat-Plan-FINAL-2.pdf) email from Merissa. It's essentially a planning grant to produce an extreme heat analysis and tree plan for Fresno. 
But first, I'm not sure if you already learned that we advanced to the interview stage of the [OPR Extreme Heat grant ](https://lci.ca.gov/climate/icarp/grants/extreme-heat-community-resilience.html)(EHCRP) that we applied for together\! Matt Holmes and Tom from CEJC did the interview yesterday (with Brent, Alec from UC Merced, and myself in attendance.) He did a great job -- fingers crossed\! I've attached the LoS that you submitted for that grant to jog your memory.   
The Fresno RFP is basically a spin-off, at a much smaller scale, of that EHCRP grant. It calls for assembling a Working Group of local leaders to meet once a month for 16 months to identify community-specific extreme heat concerns and prioritize neighborhoods for greening interventions. If interested, would you have some time for a call. The deadline is Tuesday at 5:00 PM PT. 
I've attached a drafted letter of support that sheds more light on the project. If you're not willing to serve on the Working Group but still want to be involved, you could adjust the letter to reflect interest in outreach efforts underscoring the importance of urban greening for extreme heat.   
Last thing is that we're working with Matt Holmes to find a CBO partner in either CCEJN or CCAC but he's not having much luck getting in touch. I remember you saying previously you were connected to Nayamin from CCEJN? I've also attached the LoS I wrote for her in case you are able to get in touch with her. 
Wishing you well\!
Max

# QUESTIONS
## Q\&A Fresno County Extreme Heat Analysis
[https://www.fresnocog.org/wpcontent/uploads/2024/11/QA\_FresnoCountyExtremeHeatAnalysis.pdf](https://www.fresnocog.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/QA_-Fresno-County-Extreme-Heat-Analysis.pdf)
1\. We wanted to clarify the extent of location analysis intended for Task 4: Conduct Pedestrian and Bicyclist Traffic Analysis and Location Analysis so we can best allocate resources to
match the intention. Are there preidentified potential corridors or a set number of
locations to evaluate, or shall that be determined based on public input, insight from the
ATP, and extreme heat vulnerability analysis?
There are no current preidentified potential corridors. The potential corridors to focus on
should be informed by the extreme heat vulnerability analysis, public input, and
information from the ATP. Fresno COG is also currently completing a Climate Resiliency
Plan, which includes an extreme heat risk assessment. The data from that assessment
should also inform the extreme heat vulnerability analysis and Task 4 for this project.
2\. In the 2024 ATP, it says “Fresno COG has bicycle and pedestrian counters available that
can be used to measure use of facilities. Bicycle and pedestrian counts can also be
included as part of traffic counts that are often performed when developing roadway
improvements.” I imagine these resources are from the 2015 FHWA Bike/Pedestrian Count
Technology Pilot Program Fresno COG participated in? We are wondering if you can share
more details on what is available and how those might be accessible for this project?
Fresno COG has four sets of bike/ped counters available to lend to members agencies and
various consultants who work on projects in our region. The four Pyro counters can detect
body heat so they can count pedestrians and cyclists. They are usually mounted on a pole
on the side of a trail or side of a street. However, it has been reported that vehicular traffic
can confuse the counter if mounted on the side of the street, too close to the travel lane.
The four bike counters are usually paired with the Pyro counters to get better pictures of the
active transportation participants. They use pneumatic tubes to detect bikes passing
through. These counters would be accessible to the consultant team upon request and
availability.
3\. We did not see the COG’s DBE goal in the RFP, but found a goal listed as 8.04% on the COG
website. Can you please confirm the DBE goal/requirement for this RFP?
That is correct. <comment_start id=kix.uclu50s790vm>Fresno COG’s DBE goal is 8.04% and race neutral, which means we don’t
have individual contract goals but expect overall DBE participation will meet or exceed
8.04% over a series of procurements. We encourage everyone to have DBE participation
but only require a demonstration of good faith efforts in screening and selecting DBE firms
to work with.<comment_end id=kix.uclu50s790vm>
4\. Would the COG consider an extension of the submittal deadline?
At this time, Fresno COG will not be extending the deadline for submitting proposals.

20242025 Fresno County Extreme Heat Analysis
RFP: [Fresno County Extreme Heat Analysis](https://www.fresnocog.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/RFP_Extreme-Heat-Plan-FINAL-2.pdf)
The analysis will include an extreme heat vulnerability analysis, which will provide valuable data for future climate adaptation and mitigation responses to extreme heat induced by climate change. The plan will also identify native and drought tolerant tree canopy and vegetative cover improvement projects in communities most vulnerable to extreme heat based on the extreme heat vulnerability analysis and bike and pedestrian traffic analysis.
The list of projects and strategies will inform future Regional Transportation Plan projects and climate adaptation planning for Fresno COG. The plan will also assist member agencies in additional climate adaption planning for extreme heat, general plan updates, and the design and retrofit of future and present infrastructure.
Proposal submissions are due to Jennifer Rodriguez at jrodriguez@fresnocog.org by Tuesday, November 26, 2024, by 5:00 P.M. PST

# SUMMARY
The Fresno Council of Governments (Fresno COG) is requesting proposals for an Extreme Heat Analysis in Fresno County. The project, funded by a Sustainable Transportation Planning Grant from Caltrans, focuses on assessing the impacts of extreme heat, identifying vulnerable areas, and proposing tree canopy and vegetative cover projects to mitigate these effects. This effort will support climate adaptation by informing future infrastructure projects and public health initiatives.
Key Elements of the RFP:
1.  Scope of Work:
    1.  Conduct an Extreme Heat Vulnerability Analysis to identify highrisk areas.
    2.  Perform a Pedestrian and Bicyclist Traffic Analysis to pinpoint vulnerable corridors.
    3.  Develop a list of tree canopy and vegetative cover projects targeting identified vulnerable areas.
    4.  Estimate planninglevel costs for these projects and create detailed profiles for five prioritized projects.
    5.  Draft, review, and finalize an Extreme Heat Plan incorporating public feedback.
2.  Community Engagement:
    1.  Establish a Working Group with local government, health departments, and community organizations to ensure diverse input.
    2.  Develop and implement a Public Outreach Plan to engage the community and gather feedback, with materials available in English, Spanish, and other languages as needed.
3.  Deliverables:
    1.  Working group rosters, public outreach plans, and summary memos on outreach activities.
    2.  A final Extreme Heat Vulnerability Analysis Summary Memo, detailed traffic analysis, project cost estimates, and project profiles.
    3.  A finalized plan ready for public and policy review, including presentations to relevant committees and boards.
4.  Proposal Requirements:
    1.  Submission by November 26, 2024.
    2.  Includes a transmittal letter, a detailed work plan outlining task descriptions, deliverables, a project schedule, and cost breakdowns.
    3.  Budget not to exceed $238,000, with a clear breakdown of direct, indirect, and subcontractor costs.
5.  Evaluation and Selection:
    1.  Proposals will be evaluated based on clarity, adherence to RFP requirements, management approach, and costeffectiveness. Final selection will be conducted by Fresno COG's Policy Board, and the contract is expected to be awarded by early 2025.
6.  Insurance and Compliance:
    1.  The consultant must maintain comprehensive insurance and meet all Disadvantaged Business Enterprise (DBE) requirements.
This project is critical for enhancing Fresno County’s resilience to extreme heat, particularly for disadvantaged communities that are more vulnerable to climaterelated health risks. The plan aims to integrate environmental, social, and health considerations into regional climate adaptation strategies.

<comment_start id=kix.bzpqkapdvdp>Hi Mona,<comment_end id=kix.bzpqkapdvdp>
We’re planning to apply for this [Fresno Extreme Heat Analysis](https://www.fresnocog.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/RFP_Extreme-Heat-Plan-FINAL-2.pdf) RFP, and we’d love to put together a proposal around Tree Fresno’s work and future goals. Unlike the CARB grant, we actually have a bit more time to discuss this one\! It’s due on 11/26.
We’ve been building on similar projects, like our work in [Dallas](https://texastrees.org/swmdtransformation-design/) where we designed a green corridor to protect visitors to the hospital campus from heat. Based on the framework we use for air quality and extreme heat projects, we’d love to combine your tree planting capacity with our mapping and modeling tools to prioritize locations for design interventions. Trees are a big part, but we include other strategies, too\! 
Task 1 of the RFP focuses on collaboration and community outreach to set up an Extreme Heat Working Group, and we’d want to prioritize the groups that have the best ontheground insights.
Can we schedule time this week to connect? Are you going to be at the Partners in Community Forestry Conference next week? We could plan to meet up at some point there.
Thanks,
Merissa
Hey Matt,
How have you been\!
I’m not sure if you’ve connected with Brent about this Fresno Extreme Heat Analysis rfp or the pesticide grant but I’m wondering if we could get a letter of support from VIP for Fresno?
Also, there’s a requirement to make a good faith effort to contract with local DBEs. I know Fresno is a little north of you but maybe you know of some orgs that qualify? Particularly looking for a spanish speaking facilitlation/outreach group to work with.
I’d be able to send over a draft on Monday. Let me know\!
Hi Nayamin,
I haven’t had the chance to connect with you yet, I manage the collaborations and outreach at Hyphae, hello\! We are putting in a proposal for Fresno’s Extreme Heat analysis rfp.
We’ve been building on similar projects, like our work in [Dallas](https://texastrees.org/swmdtransformation-design/) where we designed a green corridor to protect visitors to the hospital campus from heat. Based on the framework we use for air quality and extreme heat projects, we’d love to combine your tree planting capacity with our mapping and modeling tools to prioritize locations for design interventions. 
Would it be possible for CCEJN to write up a letter of support, we would send you a draft to edit.
Thank you\!
<nayamin.martinez@ccejn.org>
# Grant Checklist 
- [x] Start the Google doc (see the following [template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/13-fR-bRiDEotzHWIY_w08Kdul4s0NyjsHOl7sqqXNDc/edit?tab=t.0) for reference)
  - [x] Copypaste the standard tables from previous grants (see template) and replace the basic grant information (deadline, award amount,  of awards etc),with the new grant information as found in the RFP.
  - [x] Create a TOC.
  - [x] Below the tables, include the following categories: Questions, Action Items, Approach, Meetings, Narrative, and Budget (The budget section is merely a link to a Google Sheet) 
  - [x] Make sure these sections correspond to tabs that are visible in the lefthand column of the doc
  - [x] Make sure the doc is “pageless” (See File\>Page Setup for that setting). This allows the categories to be collapsible. 
- [x] Read carefully through the RFP
  - [ ] Read through it again
    - [ ] Identify any confusing parts
    - [ ] Identify the eligibility requirements
      - [x] Rarely, they let forprofit companies apply as the Lead
      - [ ] Most of the time, we apply on behalf of a nonprofit or CBO as technical assistants
        - [ ] If the latter, identify the partner or partners with whom we could apply
- [ ] Add grant structure explicitly into the Google Doc.
  - [ ] Turn the scoring criteria into a blank application by turning the statements of the scoring criteria into questions 
    - [ ] If the application already comes as a set of questions, then creatively embed the scoring criteria within the blank questions   
- [ ] **Grant Lift & Approach**
  - [ ] Develop strategy
    - [ ] Estimate the lift and budget and resources to produce the grant
    - [ ] Calendar Max and Merissas time and critical deadlines from now til grant deadline.
      - [ ] If you have short term proposed needs for other staff first include them on your calendar for review.
    - [ ] Identify Hyphae staff that are needed to work on the grant and why you think so and when: 
      - [ ] *MV, IH, JPG, MM etc… *
      - [ ] *use BB or DF sparingly for strategy and content (not forms)*
  - [ ] Don't proceed until you Present Strategy to BB 
    - [ ] If you can't meet with brent, show him the calendar and strategy in narrative form.
    - [ ] If brent is not available to review strategy virtually or in a meeting,
      - [ ] Turn to DF
      - [ ] IH if DF is out
  - [ ] Transfer the the lift in ClickUp & Calendar
    - [ ] Add subtasks in checklist form
    - [ ] Calendar out other staff
- [ ] Develop a draft of an approach
  - [ ] Try to meet with Brent to develop approach
  - [ ] If he is not available, share that approach with Brent and if he approves, record his suggested approach
  - [ ] Synthesize that approach and paste it into the Narrative section of the doc as well as in the Approach section (which is an abridged version)      
  - [ ] Contact partners to gauge interest and schedule meetings
  - [ ] Start pulling in previous grant material  
  - [ ] Assign sections of the proposal to each person: budget, technical sections, community engagement narrative etc
  - [ ] Calendar out when their sections need to be completed
  - [ ] Gather all relevant questions from team internally..then
    - [ ] Email grant administrator with any questions (which are then listed for reference in the Questions section)
- [ ] Simultaneously with Narrative development, you will have started the Budget 
  - [ ] Assess grant length
  - [ ] Pull budgets from AdaptOS and other relevant budgets
  - [ ] Determine basic element of scope (PM, Monitoring, Modeling, Community Engagement etc)
  - [ ] First price out the PM costs
  - [ ] Based on what’s left over after PM, price out the remaining budget elements
  - [ ] If applying with a CBO, make sure that the CBO share of the budget exceeds the Hyphae share
  - [ ] Determine how much the CBO staff wants to be paid
    - [ ] Are there benchmarks for this? (Scale: $40/hr  ?)   
- [ ] Collect Letters of Support/Commitment
- [ ] Collect required forms 
- [ ] Get CBO partners to write their sections of the Narrative
  - [ ] If they do not have capacity, ask them to submit information about their org, its legacy, and why they are best suited to be selected for the award
- [ ] Have Brent and Fletch review the document
  - [ ] Make revisions
- [ ] Have Brent and Fletch review again
  - [ ] Make revisions
- [ ] Throughout the process, upload proposal components into a Google Drive folder
  - [ ] Either merge into a single PDF or gather as Google Drive folder of distinct files, as grant guidelines indicate
- [ ] Get Final Approval from BB and DF
- [ ] Previously assign a person to compile all the grant elements into either a folder (with separate elements) or a combined PDF
- [ ] Previously assign a person to be in charge of submitting the grant.  
- [ ] Submit  
# Memento Tattoos
(Inspired by the Guy Pierce character in the Christopher Nolan movie, these are things to tattoo on your chest about grantwriting that you forget over and over despite your best efforts to remember them)
1.  Start the budget early 
2.  Start pulling in previous grant material before writing anything
3.  Don’t allow the process of collecting forms and LoS derail the writing of the Narrative and Budget 
4.  DELEGATE, GODDAMN IT, DON’T BE A HERO 
5.  Stay on the partners to get them to agree to a budget right away
Time Table Best Practices 
46 weeks: 
Prioritize partners, letters of support/forms, then budget, then narrative
3 weeks or less: 
Prioritize budget, partners/letters/forms (requires at least two dedicated days from partners), then narrative

Monthly meetings will be held to ensure consistent input and oversight of project development.\nPublic Outreach Plan Development \nOur outreach plan will employ multiple strategies to reach Fresno County's diverse communities, particularly focusing on disadvantaged communities that face disproportionate extreme heat impacts.
